  — it holds books/PDFs plus three sibling CAD repos; per your choice I scoped
  this to ToubkalCAD only.)

  What the file captures, focused on things that take reading multiple files to
  discover:

  - Build/check reality — dev (rspack on :8080), build (tsc --noEmit then
  bundle), preview, clean. Flagged that there's no test runner and no linter —
  tsc --noEmit is the only automated gate.
  - Identity gotcha — renamed AtlasCAD → ToubkalCAD; package.json and many file
  headers still say "atlascad", and there are separate sibling repos with those
  names.
  - Mandatory COOP/COEP headers for SharedArrayBuffer, and the deliberate
  file-loader (not asyncWebAssembly) WASM handling — both easy to accidentally
  break.
  - The central architecture: kernel on the main thread via window.oc; the
  worker is a partial secondary path; the 5-step create-object pipeline (Service
  → CADGeometryRegistry → store addNode → cad-add-mesh CustomEvent →
  Viewport3D); shapes live in the registry, not Zustand; the CustomEvent bus
  bridging React and the imperative Three.js scene; WasmScope for temporaries.
  - OCC API discipline — overload suffixes, where to verify signatures, and the
  pointer to docs/ROADMAP.md for verified-API corrections and feature status.

  Two things worth confirming if you want me to adjust: I deliberately did not
  document the cad.worker.ts path as primary (it implements only a handful of
  ops — tell me if you intend it to become the main path), and I left out the
  loose .txt transcript dumps in the project root since they look like scratch
  logs rather than docs.

❯ not yet, now let's return to our app, we've succeded to implement a basic     
  extrusion tool that ask for depth to create 3d solid from existing sketch,    
  now i want to go further and add advanced features to the Extrusion tool like 
  professional cad software. i have a small paper on how CATIA V5 use           
  Extrusion tool[CATIA V5                                                       
                                                                                
  In CATIA V5, the extrusion tool for solids is called the Pad command (used to 
  add material) and the Pocket command (used to remove material). It            
  transforms a 2D sketch into a 3D feature by pulling the profile along a       
  straight vector, normally perpendicular to the sketch plane.                  
  ## How the Extrusion (Pad) Tool Works                                         
                                                                                
     1. Create a Profile: Draw a 2D closed sketch (or a bounded profile) in the 
  Sketcher workbench.                                                           
     2. Select the Tool: Click the Pad icon in the Part Design workbench.       
     3. Define the Extent: Select your sketch. A preview window will appear     
  where you can enter the extrusion distance or select the bounding limits.     
     4. Confirm: Click OK to generate the solid body.                           
                                                                                
  ## List of Options Available in the Pad Tool                                  
  When the Pad Definition dialog box opens, you can customize your extrusion    
  using the following settings:                                                 
  1. Profile Definition                                                         
                                                                                
  * Selection: Defines the sketch or curves that will be extruded. Multiple     
  non-intersecting profiles within a single sketch can be selected.             
  * Sub-elements: Allows you to select specific lines/curves of a sketch to     
  extrude, rather than the entire geometry.                                     
                                                                                
  2. First & Second Limits (Extrusion End Conditions)                           
                                                                                
  * Dimension: Extrudes the sketch by an exact, manually typed numerical value. 
  * Up to Next: Extrudes the profile until it intersects with the very next     
  surface or solid body.                                                        
  * Up to Last: Extrudes the profile until it intersects the furthest possible  
  surface in the model.                                                         
  * Up to Plane: Extrudes the profile up to a selected geometric plane.         
  * Up to Surface: Extrudes the profile up to a specifically selected face or   
  surface, which will trim the extrusion.                                       
                                                                                
  3. Direction & Orientation                                                    
                                                                                
  * Normal to Profile: The default setting where the extrusion is perpendicular 
  to the sketch plane.                                                          
  * Reverse Direction: Flips the extrusion to the opposite side of the sketch   
  plane.                                                                        
  * Neutral Element / Reference: Expands the "More" options tab to allow you to 
  specify a completely different custom axis, line, or plane to dictate the     
  direction of the extrusion.                                                   
                                                                                
  4. Advanced Extent Options                                                    
                                                                                
  * Mirrored Extent: Extrudes the profile in both directions symmetrically from 
  the sketch plane by the same distance.                                        
  * Thick: Allows you to take an open profile (or a sketch with thickness       
  parameters) and turn it into a 3D solid wall with custom inside/outside       
  thicknesses.                                                                  
                                                                                
  5. Variations of the Tool                                                     
                                                                                
  * Multi-Pad: Lets you extrude multiple, individual profiles from the same     
  sketch at different lengths or in different directions all in one command.    
  * Drafted Filleted Pad: Combines padding, edge fillets, and face drafts into  
  a single streamlined step.                                                    
                                                                                
  6. Draft                                                                      
  Adds an inward or outward angle (draft) to the extruded profile (e.g., 10° or 
  -5°) to create sloped or conical shapes.                                      
  ]. i searched for similar features in OPENCASCADE, here what i found          
  [OPENCASCADE:                                                                 
                                                                                
  To perform solid extrusions similar to CATIA V5's Pad and Pocket tools,       
  OpenCascade.js relies on the BRepPrimAPI and BRepAlgoAPI packages.            
  OpenCascade treats extrusion as a "sweeping" operation where a 2D wire or     
  face is swept along a 3D vector.                                              
  Here are the primary functions and classes in OpenCascade.js that mirror      
  CATIA's extrusion capabilities:                                               
  ## 1. Basic Extrusion (The "Pad" Equivalent)                                  
  To extrude a 2D profile along a straight vector, you use the linear sweeping  
  class.                                                                        
                                                                                
  * BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism: This is the core function for standard extrusions.   
  You pass it a base shape (like a face or a wire) and a 3D vector (gp_Vec)     
  that defines both the direction and the exact length of the extrusion.        
                                                                                
  ## 2. Material Removal (The "Pocket" Equivalent)                              
  OpenCascade does not have a dedicated "remove material" extrusion tool.       
  Instead, it uses a two-step Boolean approach.                                 
                                                                                
     1. Use BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism to create a solid extrusion of your pocket    
  profile.                                                                      
     2. BRepAlgoAPI_Cut: Use this Boolean subtraction function to cut the newly 
  created prism solid out of your main solid body.                              
                                                                                
  ## 3. Advanced Extrusion Options                                              
  OpenCascade.js replicates CATIA's advanced limits and directions through      
  specific programming workflows:                                               
                                                                                
  * Custom Direction: By default, BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism lets you define any     
  arbitrary directional vector (gp_Vec), making it easy to extrude at           
  non-perpendicular angles.                                                     
  * Thick / Thin Wall Extrusion: If you pass an open wire (instead of a closed  
  face) into BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism, it generates a shell (a hollow surface). To 
  give it thickness like CATIA's "Thick" option, you pass that shell into       
  BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid.                                                 
  * Up to Next / Surface Limits: OpenCascade does not have a single click "Up   
  to Next" button. To achieve this, you must extrude your profile intentionally 
  past the target surface, and then use BRepAlgoAPI_Section or                  
  BRepAlgoAPI_Common to trim or intersect the extrusion precisely with your     
  bounding geometry.                                                            
                                                                                
  To build a universal extrusion engine in OpenCascade.js that mirrors CATIA    
  V5, your  will need to orchestrate specific workflows using OpenCascade’s     
  geometric and topological classes. Since you handle everything via            
  TopoDS_Face, here are the exact architecture logic, mechanism steps, and      
  classes required for each advanced option.                                    
  ------------------------------                                                
  ## 1. The Core Extrusions (Dimension & Symmetric)                             
  For basic length-based extents, you stick to linear sweeping.                 
                                                                                
  * Standard Dimension:                                                         
  * Mechanism: Create a 3D vector representing direction and distance. Sweep    
  the face along it.                                                            
     * Classes: gp_Vec (direction & length) → BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism.            
  * Symmetric / Mirrored Extent:                                                
  * Mechanism: Perform two separate extrusions from the same face—one along the 
  positive vector, one along the inverted negative vector. Then, fuse them      
  together into a single solid.                                                 
     * Classes:                                                                 
     * gp_Vec.Reversed() to invert the direction.                               
        * BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism (run twice).                                    
        * BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse to combine both halves into one seamless             
  TopoDS_Shape.                                                                 
                                                                                
  ------------------------------                                                
  ## 2. Bounded Extrusions (Up to Face / Up to Next / Up to Last)               
  OpenCascade does not have an "Up to Face" constraint natively. Your AI must   
  use an "Extrude-and-Trim" workflow.                                           
                                                                                
  * Mechanism for "Up to Face":                                                 
  1. Over-Extrude: Intentionally calculate a depth that guarantees the prism    
  completely intersects and passes through the target bounding face/solid.      
     2. Split/Trim: Use a Boolean operation to section or split the             
  over-extruded solid using the target bounding surface.                        
     3. Filter: Analyze the resulting solid pieces and keep only the piece      
  connected to your original sketch plane.                                      
  * Classes:                                                                    
     * BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism (for the oversized solid).                         
        * BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter (ideal for cutting a solid with a face/surface   
  without losing the sub-shapes).                                               
        * TopExp_Explorer (to iterate through the split pieces to find the      
  valid solid).                                                                 
     * Mechanism for "Up to Next" / "Up to Last":                               
  * Mechanism: Over-extrude the sketch through the entire existing 3D model.    
  Use a Boolean intersection to find where the extrusion meets the existing     
  solid, then isolate the first or last intersecting volume.                    
     * Classes: BRepAlgoAPI_Common (tracks the intersection volume) or          
  BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter.                                                         
                                                                                
  ------------------------------                                                
  ## 3. Material Operations (Pad vs. Pocket)                                    
  Whether you add or remove material depends entirely on Boolean mathematics    
  after the solid extrusion is generated.                                       
                                                                                
  * Pad (Add Material):                                                         
  * Mechanism: Standard solid fusion.                                           
     * Classes: BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse (combines the new extrusion with your main     
  workspace solid).                                                             
  * Pocket (Remove Material):                                                   
  * Mechanism: Standard solid subtraction.                                      
     * Classes: BRepAlgoAPI_Cut (subtracts the new extrusion from your main     
  workspace solid).                                                             
                                                                                
  ------------------------------                                                
  ## 4. Thin Wall / Hollow Extrusions (Thick)                                   
  CATIA’s "Thick" option adds a wall thickness to the profile. In OpenCascade,  
  this depends on whether the sketch is open or closed.                         
                                                                                
  * Case A: Open Sketch (Wires to Thick Walls):                                 
  * Mechanism: Extruding an open wire yields a hollow sheet (shell). You must   
  apply an offset to that sheet to give it a solid thickness.                   
     * Classes: BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism (creates a TopoDS_Shell) →                
  BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid (hollows/offsets the shell into a solid).        
  * Case B: Closed Sketch (Hollow/Shelled Solid):                               
  * Mechanism: Extrude the closed face into a standard solid, then hollow out   
  the inside while leaving specific faces open.                                 
     * Classes: BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism → TopTools_ListOfShape (to store faces    
  you want to remove/hollow out) → BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid (generates the  
  final shelled solid).                                                         
                                                                                
  ------------------------------                                                
  ## Summary Architecture Cheat-Sheet for Your AI                               
  Provide your AI with this structural map to write your state handlers:        
                                                                                
  | CATIA Feature | OpenCascade.js Approach | Key Classes to Use |              
  |---|---|---|                                                                 
  | Dimension | Direct linear sweep | gp_Vec, BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism |           
  | Mirrored Extent | Double sweep + Union | gp_Vec.Reversed(),                 
  BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse |                                                            
  | Pocket | Boolean Subtraction | BRepAlgoAPI_Cut |                            
  | Pad | Boolean Addition | BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse |                                 
  | Up to Face | Over-extrude + Split + Filter | BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter,          
  TopExp_Explorer |                                                             
  | Thick | Shell generation + Offset | BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid |          
                                                                                
  To help you build the exact filtering logic for the "Up to Face"              
  (Over-extrude + Split + Filter) workflow, it will need to navigate the        
  resulting topological shapes. When you use a class like BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter, 
  it breaks the oversized solid into multiple independent TopoDS_Solid pieces.  
  ]. check this and give me roadmap to begin implementing extended feature in   
  our basic extrussion tool. act as a 3d scad senior developer and propose      
  advanced feature for this important tool

● I've read the current extrusion path end-to-end. Here's my assessment and a
  concrete roadmap, as a senior CAD dev.

  Where we are today

  The current tool is a clean but minimal "blind extrude":

  - OccExtrusionService.extrudeWireToSolid — closed planar wire →
  BRepBuilderAPI_MakeFace_15 → single BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism_1(face, vec, false, 
  true). One direction (= workplane normal), height > 0 only, closed profiles
  only.
  - Op3DPanel (shared with revolve/loft/sweep/fillet/chamfer) exposes a single
  Height slider. Params persist as node.params.opParams = { h }; re-edit re-runs
  computeShape. Live debounced preview already works.
  - The result is a standalone solid node (parentId: null); the source sketch
  gets nested under it via adoptSketchSources.

  So vs. CATIA Pad, we have only First Limit = Dimension, Normal-to-profile, no 
  boolean. Everything else (second limit, up-to-*, mirrored, thick, draft,
  Pad/Pocket) is missing.

  Corrections to the research you pasted

  Mostly sound, but three things would bite you if implemented literally:

  1. Mirrored extent — don't "extrude twice + Fuse." That creates a
  coincident-face seam the fuser has to heal (slow, occasionally non-manifold).
  The robust trick is a single prism: translate the base face by -h/2 along the
  normal (gp_Trsf + BRepBuilderAPI_Transform), then MakePrism by the full h. One
  solid, no boolean. Same logic gives asymmetric two-sided (translate by -h2,
  extrude h1+h2).
  2. Draft — the summary's class list is wrong/missing. There is no draft option
  on MakePrism. Use BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle as a post-process: build the
  straight prism, then add draft to its lateral faces relative to a neutral
  plane (the sketch plane). This is also our roadmap's M3.
  (BRepOffsetAPI_MakeDraft is the sweep-a-wire-with-taper alternative — messier
  for our wire→solid flow.)
  3. Pad/Pocket needs a target body, which our data model doesn't have. We keep
  every solid as an independent root node — there's no "active body." So Pad
  (Fuse) / Pocket (Cut) requires picking which solid to combine with. Good news:
  we already have that infra — OccBooleanService.fuse/subtract +
  useCADBooleanPick + BooleanActionPanel. The first feature in an empty doc is
  just "New" (no boolean).

  Everything else (over-extrude + BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter + TopExp_Explorer filter
  for Up-to-*; BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid for Thick) is correct and matches
  our docs/ROADMAP.md tracks M1–M4.

  The keystone: extend the params schema first

  Every feature below is a field on the extrude params, not a new tool.
  Backward-compatible superset of { h }:

  interface ExtrudeParams {
    h: number;                       // First Limit distance (existing)
    endType: 'blind' | 'symmetric' | 'twoSided'
           | 'upToNext' | 'upToLast' | 'upToPlane' | 'upToFace';
    h2?: number;                     // Second Limit (twoSided)
    reverse?: boolean;               // flip along normal
    draftAngle?: number;             // degrees, BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle
    thick?: { inner: number; outer: number };  // BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid
    operation?: 'new' | 'pad' | 'pocket';       // boolean intent
    targetSolidId?: string;          // for pad/pocket
    limitRef?: { kind: 'plane' | 'face'; id: string };  // up-to targets
  }

  computeShape's extrude branch becomes a small pipeline: profile → (translate 
  for symmetric/two-sided) → prism / up-to-trim → draft → thick → boolean. Each
  stage is independently testable and each maps to one roadmap phase.

  Phased roadmap (dependency-ordered)

  Phase: E1
  Feature: Reverse + Two-sided/Symmetric limits
  OCC classes: gp_Trsf, BRepBuilderAPI_Transform, MakePrism
  Effort: S
  Notes: Pure single-prism math; no new infra. Best first win.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: E2
  Feature: Pad / Pocket (boolean intent)
  OCC classes: BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse/_Cut (have it), useCADBooleanPick
  Effort: M
  Notes: Adds New/Add/Remove + target-solid pick. Unlocks "real" modeling.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: E3
  Feature: Thick (thin-wall)
  OCC classes: BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid; open-wire path + 2D
    BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset for closed
  Effort: M
  Notes: Needs open-profile support (current code rejects open wires).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: E4
  Feature: Draft angle
  OCC classes: BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle, TopExp_Explorer (side faces)
  Effort: M
  Notes: Post-process the prism; neutral plane = sketch plane.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: E5
  Feature: Up-to-Plane / Up-to-Face
  OCC classes: BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter, TopExp_Explorer, face pick
    (OccFaceService.extractPlanarFaces exists)
  Effort: L
  Notes: Over-extrude → split → keep piece touching sketch plane.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: E6
  Feature: Up-to-Next / Up-to-Last
  OCC classes: BRepAlgoAPI_Common/Splitter over existing bodies
  Effort: L
  Notes: Builds on E5's filter logic.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: E7
  Feature: Multi-profile / Multi-Pad
  OCC classes: region detection per profile (SketchRegions exists), per-profile
    depth
  Effort: L
  Notes: Multiple non-intersecting profiles in one sketch.

  Effort: S ≈ half-day, M ≈ 1–2 days, L ≈ 3+ days. UI work for E1–E4 is
  conditional rows in Op3DPanel's extrude branch (end-condition dropdown,
  reverse toggle, thick/draft toggles). E2 and E5/E6 add a pick step, reusing
  the boolean/face-pick hooks.

  What I'd build first

  E1 + E2 together is the highest value-to-effort jump: E1 is almost pure
  geometry math (no new UI infra), and E2 turns the tool from "spawns
  disconnected blobs" into actual additive/subtractive part modeling using infra
  you already have. E3 (Thick) and E4 (Draft) are independent and can follow in
  any order. E5–E7 are the heavy, deferrable tail.

  One thing worth confirming before I start, since it shapes E2's UX:

        2  // ToubkalCAD – OccExtrusionService.ts
        3  // Wire (3D, on any plane) → Face → Solid via BRepPrimAPI_Make
           Prism.
        4  // Extrusion direction = workplane normal (retrieved from node
            params).
        5 +//                                                            
        6 +// End conditions (CATIA "Pad" First/Second Limit, single-pris
          +m approach):                                                  
        7 +//   • blind     — extrude `height` along the (optionally reve
          +rsed) normal.                                                 
        8 +//   • symmetric — centred on the sketch plane: shift the face
          + back by                                                      
        9 +//                 height/2, then extrude the full `height`. O
          +ne prism, no                                                  
       10 +//                 boolean seam (more robust than extrude-twic
          +e + fuse).                                                    
       11 +//   • twoSided  — asymmetric two-way: shift back by `height2`
          +, extrude                                                     
       12 +//                 `height + height2`.                        
       13  // ===========================================================
           =
       14  
       15 +import { WasmScope } from '../utils/WasmScope';               
       16 +                                                              
       17 +export type ExtrudeEnd = 'blind' | 'symmetric' | 'twoSided';  
       18 +                                                              
       19 +export interface ExtrudeOptions {                             
       20 +  /** First-limit distance along the (optionally reversed) dir
          +ection. > 0. */                                               
       21 +  height:     number;                                         
       22 +  /** End condition. Default 'blind'. */                      
       23 +  end?:       ExtrudeEnd;                                     
       24 +  /** Second-limit distance for 'twoSided' (back side). > 0 wh
          +en used. */                                                   
       25 +  height2?:   number;                                         
       26 +  /** Flip the extrusion to the opposite side of the sketch pl
          +ane. */                                                       
       27 +  reverse?:   boolean;                                        
       28 +  /** Unit normal of the sketch plane. Defaults to [0,1,0] (Y-
          +up). */                                                       
       29 +  direction?: [number, number, number];                       
       30 +}                                                             
       31 +                                                              
       32  export class OccExtrusionService {
       33    /**
        9 -   * Extrude a closed planar 3D wire into a solid.            
       34 +   * Extrude a closed planar 3D wire into a solid with CATIA-s
          +tyle end                                                      
       35 +   * conditions. Returns a TopoDS_Solid owned by the caller (r
          +egister it in                                                 
       36 +   * CADGeometryRegistry).                                    
       37 +   */                                                         
       38 +  static extrude(oc: any, wire: any, opts: ExtrudeOptions): an
          +y {                                                           
       39 +    const {                                                   
       40 +      height,                                                 
       41 +      end       = 'blind',                                    
       42 +      height2   = 0,                                          
       43 +      reverse   = false,                                      
       44 +      direction = [0, 1, 0],                                  
       45 +    } = opts;                                                 
       46 +                                                              
       47 +    if (height <= 0) throw new Error('Extrusion height must be
          + > 0');                                                       
       48 +                                                              
       49 +    // Normalise direction (and flip if reversed).            
       50 +    let [dx, dy, dz] = direction;                             
       51 +    const len = Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);       
       52 +    if (len < 1e-10) throw new Error('Extrusion direction is z
          +ero-length');                                                 
       53 +    dx /= len; dy /= len; dz /= len;                          
       54 +    if (reverse) { dx = -dx; dy = -dy; dz = -dz; }            
       55 +                                                              
       56 +    // Resolve back-offset + total length from the end conditi
          +on.                                                           
       57 +    let back  = 0;          // distance to shift the face back
          +wards before sweeping                                         
       58 +    let total = height;     // full sweep length              
       59 +    if (end === 'symmetric') {                                
       60 +      back  = height / 2;                                     
       61 +      total = height;                                         
       62 +    } else if (end === 'twoSided') {                          
       63 +      if (height2 <= 0) throw new Error('Second limit (height2
          +) must be > 0');                                              
       64 +      back  = height2;                                        
       65 +      total = height + height2;                               
       66 +    }                                                         
       67 +                                                              
       68 +    const scope = new WasmScope();                            
       69 +    try {                                                     
       70 +      // Build face from the closed planar wire.              
       71 +      const faceMaker = scope.keep(new oc.BRepBuilderAPI_MakeF
          +ace_15(wire, true));                                          
       72 +      if (!faceMaker.IsDone()) {                              
       73 +        throw new Error(                                      
       74 +          'Extrusion: face creation failed — wire must be clos
          +ed and planar. ' +                                            
       75 +          'Only Circle, Rectangle, Ellipse, Polygon and Rounde
          +d-Rectangle can be extruded.',                                
       76 +        );                                                    
       77 +      }                                                       
       78 +      let face = faceMaker.Shape();                           
       79 +                                                              
       80 +      // Shift the face backwards for symmetric / two-sided sw
          +eeps.                                                         
       81 +      if (back > 1e-9) {                                      
       82 +        const trsf = scope.keep(new oc.gp_Trsf_1());          
       83 +        const tv   = scope.keep(new oc.gp_Vec_4(-dx * back, -d
          +y * back, -dz * back));                                       
       84 +        trsf.SetTranslation_1(tv);                            
       85 +        const mover = scope.keep(new oc.BRepBuilderAPI_Transfo
          +rm_2(face, trsf, true));                                      
       86 +        if (!mover.IsDone()) throw new Error('Extrusion: face 
          +offset failed');                                              
       87 +        face = mover.Shape();                                 
       88 +      }                                                       
       89 +                                                              
       90 +      const vec   = scope.keep(new oc.gp_Vec_4(dx * total, dy 
          +* total, dz * total));                                        
       91 +      const prism = scope.keep(new oc.BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism_1(
          +face, vec, false, true));                                     
       92 +      if (!prism.IsDone()) throw new Error('Extrusion: prism c
          +omputation failed');                                          
       93 +      return prism.Shape();                                   
       94 +    } finally {                                               
       95 +      scope.free();                                           
       96 +    }                                                         
       97 +  }                                                           
       98 +                                                              
       99 +  /**                                                         
      100 +   * Backwards-compatible blind extrusion.                    
      101     * @param direction  Unit normal of the sketch plane (extrus
           ion axis).
       11 -   *                   Defaults to [0,1,0] (Y-up) for backward
          -s compat.                                                     
      102     */
      103    static extrudeWireToSolid(

● Done. E1 + E2 are implemented, and I routed the Ribbon's Extrude button onto
  the full panel so the advanced controls are on the primary entry point. All
  static gates pass: tsc --noEmit clean, production build succeeds, dev server
  boots and serves 200.

  What changed

  E1 — End conditions + reverse (OccExtrusionService.ts)
  - New extrude(oc, wire, opts) with end: 'blind' | 'symmetric' | 'twoSided',
  height2, and reverse. Symmetric/two-sided use the single-prism trick (shift
  the face back via gp_Trsf, sweep the full length) — no double-extrude + fuse
  seam. Old extrudeWire/extrudeWireToSolid kept as thin wrappers, so nothing
  else breaks.

  E2 — Pad / Pocket with explicit pick
  - OccExtrusionService produces the prism; computeShape then
  OccBooleanService.fuse (Pad) or .subtract (Pocket) against the picked target.
  Cut(target, prism) / Fuse(target, prism) ordering verified.
  - New EXTRUDE_TARGET_PICK interaction mode + useCADExtrudeTargetPick hook
  (mounted in Viewport3D): hover-highlights candidate solids green, one-shot
  click selects, excludes sketches/wires and the in-progress node. Mirrors the
  existing boolean-pick pattern.
  - Store: op3DTargetPick one-shot field +
  startOp3DTargetPick/setOp3DTargetPick.
  - On commit, the consumed target is hidden (not deleted) so only the combined
  body shows — reversible since the node stays in the tree.

  UI (Op3DPanel.tsx): extrude branch now has Limit (Blind/Sym/2-Sided), Limit 1
  (+ Limit 2 when two-sided), Reverse, Result (New/Pad/Pocket), and a Target
  picker row. Live preview shows the combined boolean result and hides the
  overlapped committed meshes while previewing. Params + target persist on the
  node, so re-edit restores them.

  Honest scope notes

  - I verified compile/build/boot, but not the actual geometry interactively —
  that needs drawing a sketch and clicking through with the live WASM kernel,
  which I can't drive headlessly here. Worth a manual pass: sketch → Extrude →
  try Sym/2-Sided/Reverse, then Result=Pocket, pick another solid.
  - v1 limitation: if you re-edit a Pad/Pocket back to "New", the
  previously-hidden target isn't auto-re-shown (it stays in the tree, toggle it
  visible). Fixable later; flagged rather than hidden.
  - E3–E7 (Thick, Draft, Up-to-Face/Next/Last, Multi-pad) remain as scoped in
  the roadmap.

  Want me to (a) record the E1–E7 roadmap into docs/ROADMAP.md, (b) do an
  in-browser verification pass with screenshots, or (c) move on to E3 (Thick) /
  E4 (Draft)?

● Done. The test script is written, wired into npm, and passing.

  What you got

  scripts/test-extrude.mjs — a headless geometry test. Run it with:

  npm run test:extrude      # or: node scripts/test-extrude.mjs

  It boots the real OpenCascade WASM kernel in Node (via
  opencascade.js/dist/node.js) and asserts volume + centroid-Z invariants for
  every E1/E2 case:

  ┌─────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬────────┐
  │          Case           │         Expectation          │ Result │
  ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ E1 blind h=10           │ box z[0,10], vol 1000, cz 5  │ ✓      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ E1 reverse              │ box z[-10,0], cz −5          │ ✓      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ E1 symmetric            │ box z[-5,5], cz 0            │ ✓      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ E1 two-sided h=10/h2=4  │ box z[-4,10], vol 1400, cz 3 │ ✓      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ E2 pocket (4×4 through) │ 1000 − 160 = 840             │ ✓      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ E2 pad (block on top)   │ 1000 + 1000 = 2000           │ ✓      │
  └─────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┴────────┘

  7 passed, 0 failed, exits non-zero on any failure (CI-friendly).

  Why this design

  The script's extrude() / fuse() / subtract() helpers mirror the exact OCC call
  sequences in OccExtrusionService.extrude and OccBooleanService, so it
  validates the geometry core the UI drives — the single-prism centering math,
  reverse, two-sided length, and the Pad/Pocket boolean ordering. I used volume
  + centroid from GProp_GProps (exact geometry, not tessellated) rather than a
  bounding box, because the Bnd_Box out-param path threw under Node while GProp
  is rock-solid there (and centroid-Z alone distinguishes all four end
  conditions).

  Two honest caveats

  - Scope: this tests the kernel, not the React/Three.js/picking layer. The
  target-pick hook, live preview, and "hide consumed target" behavior still need
  an in-browser pass (sketch → Extrude → toggle Sym/2-Sided/Reverse →
  Result=Pocket → click a solid).
  - Faithful-mirror, not direct-import: the helpers are ported (and commented as
  such) rather than importing the .ts services, because their unextended
  relative imports don't resolve under Node's type-stripping without a build
  step. If you'd prefer a zero-drift version, I can add a tiny tsc pre-compile
  so the test imports the actual service modules — say the word.

  Nothing committed yet. Want me to do the in-browser verification pass, or add
  the direct-import variant?

  and the status bar still shows EXTRUDE_TARGET_PICK — Playwright's synthetic
  trusted clicks aren't reaching the Three.js raycast listeners (same reason the
  sketch pixel-clicks failed; something intercepts hit-testing, which is why
  the overlay path worked). The pick mode activates correctly, but the click
  doesn't land.

  — this exercises the real useCADExtrudeTargetPick raycast/filter/store path,
  just bypassing the hit-test layer that's eating trusted clicks.

● Verification: E1 (extrude end conditions + reverse) & E2 (Pad/Pocket target 
  pick)

  Verdict: PASS (E1 fully observed in-browser; E2 UI + mode + validation-guard
  observed in-browser, successful-boolean step not driveable under headless
  synthetic input — explained below)

  Claim: The Ribbon Extrude button opens the rich Op3DPanel (via
  createAndEditOp) with new E1 controls — Limit (Blind/Sym/2-Sided), Limit 1/2
  sliders, Reverse — and E2 controls — Result (New/Pad/Pocket) + a viewport
  target-solid picker that cuts/fuses the extrusion against a clicked solid and
  hides the consumed target. Matches the diff (Op3DPanel.tsx,
  OccExtrusionService.ts, Ribbon.tsx, useCADExtrudeTargetPick.ts, cadStore.ts).

  Method: Cold start — no repo verifier. Built dist/ fresh, then drove the real
  app in headless Chrome via Playwright (playwright-core 1.60 + system chrome).
  The dev server kept getting SIGTERM'd by the sandbox, so I served dist/ from
  inside the Playwright process via request interception with COOP/COEP headers
  — confirmed window.crossOriginIsolated === true, OCC WASM kernel loaded ("WASM
  kernel loaded", status bar "OCC ✓").

  Steps (driving the running app)

  1. ✅ Loaded app → kernel ready, scene empty. 01_loaded.png
  2. ✅ Model tab → Box → solid created (9e18b7, a 10×10×10 target body).
  03_box_made.png
  3. ✅ Sketch tab → Rect → plane selector (ZX) → drew rectangle via the app's
  coordinate-entry overlay (corners (0,0)→(40,30)) → sketch wire created
  (205006). 05_sketch_drawn.png
  4. ✅ Model tab → selected "Sketch 1" → Extrude → rich panel opened in EDIT
  mode; tree shows Extrusion1 with sketch nested. Panel renders LIMIT 
  [Blind|Sym|2-Sided], LIMIT 1 slider, REVERSE [Off|On], RESULT 
  [New|Pad|Pocket]. 07_extrude_panel.png
  5. ✅ Toggled 2-Sided → conditional LIMIT 2 (MM) slider appeared (only in
  two-sided); preview updated. 09_twosided.png
  6. ✅ Toggled Reverse=On → preview re-rendered. 11_reverse.png
  7. ✅ E2: Result=Pocket → a TARGET row appeared; clicked Pick target → status
  bar switched to EXTRUDE_TARGET_PICK, button → "Click a solid…". 13_picking.png

● Here's the manual click-through to confirm E2 end-to-end. It takes ~2 minutes.

  0. Start the app (in your own terminal, not via me)

  cd ~/Documents/projects/ToubkalCAD && npm run dev

  Open http://localhost:8080 and wait for the console line "ToubkalCAD ready —
  WASM kernel loaded" (status bar shows OCC ✓).

  ---
  1. E1 sanity check (30 s, no target needed)

  1. Sketch tab → Rect → in the plane dialog click Sketch on Plane (ZX is fine).
  2. Click two points in the viewport to draw a rectangle.
  3. Top-right → Quit Sketch.
  4. Model tab → click the sketch in the left Model Tree → click Extrude.
  5. In the panel, watch the live preview as you:
    - Toggle Limit → Sym (solid centers on the plane), → 2-Sided (a second Limit
  2 slider appears), → Blind.
    - Toggle Reverse → On (solid flips to the other side).
  6. Leave Result = New, click Apply ✓. → Extrusion1 appears.

  ✅ E1 confirmed if the preview changed with each toggle and the solid
  committed.

  ---
  2. E2 Pocket — the boolean (the main test)

  Set up an overlapping pair so the cut is visible:

  1. Model tab → Box → accept the default (10×10×10) → Box appears at the
  origin.
  2. Sketch tab → Rect → Sketch on Plane (ZX — this is the box's bottom plane).
  3. Draw a smaller rectangle inside the box footprint. For a guaranteed-clean
  result, use the bottom coordinate-entry bar: corner 1 X=2 Y=2 → →, corner 2
  X=8 Y=8 → →. (Or just click two points well inside the box.)
  4. Quit Sketch → Model tab → select the new sketch in the tree → Extrude.
  5. In the panel:
    - Set Limit 1 = 12 (taller than the 10 box, so it passes through).
    - Result → Pocket.
    - Click Pick target (button turns to "Click a solid…", status bar shows
  EXTRUDE_TARGET_PICK).
    - Hover the box → it highlights green → click it. The Target row should now
  show "Box 10×10×10" and the mode returns to Select.
    - Click Update ✓.

  ✅ Pocket confirmed if:
  - The box now has a square hole punched through it (the extruded profile
  subtracted).
  - In the Model Tree, the original Box node is now hidden (greyed eye-icon) —
  it was consumed.
  - Optional hard proof: select the result, open Properties → Measure (right
  panel) and check the volume dropped below the solid box (1000 − the punched
  column).

  ▎ Negative-path check (optional): with Result=Pocket selected, click Update 
  ▎ before picking a target — you should get the red error "Pick a Pocket 
  ▎ (Remove) target solid first" and the panel stays open. (I verified this one 
  ▎ in headless.)

  ---
  3. E2 Pad (optional, ~30 s)

  Repeat step 2 but draw the rectangle overlapping the top of the box, set
  Result → Pad, pick the box → the new extrusion fuses onto it (one combined
  body; box hidden).

  ---

  (step 5's green-highlight pick). If hovering highlights the box green and

  see and I'll dig into useCADExtrudeTargetPick.

※ recap: Goal: add CATIA-style advanced features to ToubkalCAD's extrude tool. E1 (end conditions/reverse) and E2 (Pad/Pocket) are built, tested, and roadmap-recorded. Next: you run the manual click-through to confirm the E2 target-pick works in-browser. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ it's ok, meanwhile volume in properties->Analysis show only Box and           
  extrusion, how can i select result                                            

● There's no separate "Result" node — that's by design, and it's the source of
  the confusion.

  For Pad/Pocket, the boolean output is written into the Extrusion node itself,
  and the Box (the target) is hidden because it's been consumed. So:

  - Select the Extrusion node in the Model Tree → Properties → Analysis. That
  volume is the result (box cut/fused with the prism).
  - The Box node still shows its original volume (1000) because its own registry
  shape is untouched — it's just hidden, not modified. So "Box = 1000,
  Extrusion = combined" is exactly what you'd expect to see.

  Confirm the cut actually happened (the key check)

  Look at the Extrusion's Analysis volume:
  - Pocket worked → Extrusion volume = box − punched column (e.g. box 1000 minus
  a 6×6 column through it = 1000 − 6·6·10 = 640).
  - Boolean did not apply → Extrusion volume = just the prism (≈ profile area ×
  Limit 1), and the Box node is still visible (not greyed) in the tree.

  That visible-vs-hidden Box is your quickest tell: if the Box's eye-icon is 
  greyed/off, the Pocket fused; if the Box is still shown, the target pick 
  didn't register and you got a plain "New" extrusion.

  If you tell me the box size, your rectangle corners, and the Limit 1 value,
  plus the Extrusion volume you're seeing, I'll give you the exact expected
  number so you can confirm the cut numerically.

  One honest note: a user reasonably expects a single "Pocket1" result body, not
  "the Box vanished and the Extrusion secretly became the combined solid."
  That's the v1 limitation I flagged (no feature tree yet). A cleaner version
  would rename the result (e.g. Pocket1) and nest both the Box and the sketch
  under it — worth doing in the E-track follow-up if the current behavior feels
  off.

● E4 (Draft angle) is done and verified.

  What I added

  OccExtrusionService.applyDraft — a post-prism stage that tapers the lateral
  walls:
  - Iterates faces (TopExp_Explorer), selects planar side walls (normal ⟂ pull
  direction — caps are skipped), and drafts them about the neutral = sketch 
  plane (gp_Pln_3(origin, pullDir)) via BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle_2.
  - Slots into the pipeline as profile → prism → draft → boolean, so draft
  composes with Pad/Pocket and the end conditions automatically.
  - Gracefully no-ops if nothing is draftable, and throws a clear error if the
  angle is too steep for the geometry.

  UI: a Draft (°) slider (−30…30) in the Extrude panel, between Reverse and
  Result. Persists with the other params for re-edit.

  Verification

  Extended the headless harness (npm run test:extrude) with an E4 case — 8/8 
  pass:

  E4 — draft / taper
    ✓ 10° draft → square frustum (inward)  (vol=688.80 frustum=1.000)
       vol=688.80  (straight=1000, inward≈688.8, outward≈1394.1)

  A 10° draft on a 10³ box produces a square frustum of volume 688.80, matching
  the pyramidal-frustum formula exactly. This also pinned down the sign 
  convention: positive angle narrows toward the pull/extrusion direction (inward
  taper). tsc clean, production build clean.

  Scope notes

  - Planar walls only this pass — circular profiles (cylindrical walls) are left
  straight; BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle can taper them too, but they need a
  different face-selection test. Noted in the roadmap and code.
  - Roadmap updated: E4 ✅ (2026-06-08), recommended sequence now shows E1→E2 +
  E4 done, E3 Thick as the next target.

  Remaining E-track: E3 (Thick/thin-wall) — the one that needs the open-wire
  refactor — then E5/E6 (Up-to-* limits) and E7 (multi-profile). Want me to keep
  going with E3, or pause here? Note there's a growing pile of uncommitted work
  (E1/E2/E4 + roadmap + test + regenerated dist/) — happy to commit it on a
  branch whenever you want.

      125 +  private static applyThickWall(                              
      126 +    oc:        any,                                           
      127 +    solid:     any,                                           
      128 +    thickness: number,                                        
      129 +    pullDir:   [number, number, number],                      
      130 +  ): any {                                                    
      131 +    const [px, py, pz] = pullDir;                             
      132 +    const scope = new WasmScope();                            
      133 +    try {                                                     
      134 +      // Collect the cap faces (planar, normal ∥ pull directio
          +n) to open.                                                   
      135 +      const caps = scope.keep(new oc.TopTools_ListOfShape_1())
          +;

      137 +        solid, oc.TopAbs_ShapeEnum.TopAbs_FACE, oc.TopAbs_Shap
          +eEnum.TopAbs_SHAPE,                                           
      138 +      ));                                                     
      139 +      while (exp.More()) {

          +face_2(surfH));

          +eomAbs_Plane) {

          +ne()).Axis()).Direction();                                    
      148 +              const dot  = Math.abs(nDir.X() * px + nDir.Y() *
          + py + nDir.Z() * pz);                                         
      149 +              if (dot > 0.5) caps.Append_1(face);  // cap → re
          +move (list keeps its own ref)                                 
      150 +            }                                                 
      151 +          }                                                   
      152 +        } finally { fscope.free(); }                          
      153 +        exp.Next();                                           
      154 +      }                                                       
      155 +      if (caps.Extent() === 0) throw new Error('Thick: no cap 
          +face found to open the wall.');                               
      156 +

          +id());

      161 +        oc.GeomAbs_JoinType.GeomAbs_Intersection, false,      
      162 +        new oc.Message_ProgressRange_1(),                     
      163 +      );                                                      
      164 +      mts.Build(new oc.Message_ProgressRange_1());            
      165 +      if (!mts.IsDone()) throw new Error('Thick: wall failed —
          + thickness too large for the profile?');                      
      166 +      return mts.Shape();                                     
      167 +    } finally {

      169 +    }

● E3 (Thick / thin-wall) is done and verified.

  What I added

  OccExtrusionService.applyThickWall — hollows the prism into an open tube:
  - Collects the cap faces (planar, normal ∥ pull direction — reusing the draft
  cap-detection), then BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid.MakeThickSolidByJoin(solid, 
  caps, −t, …) offsets the side walls inward by thickness, leaving both ends
  open.
  - Slots into the pipeline as prism → draft → thick, so Wall composes with end
  conditions, draft, and the Pad/Pocket boolean automatically.
  - Throws clear errors if no cap is found or the wall is too thick for the
  profile.
  - This is also the M1 Shell foundation (same OCC machinery).

  UI: a Wall (mm) slider (0–20) in the Extrude panel; 0 = solid.

  Verification

  Extended npm run test:extrude with an E3 case — 9/9 pass:

  E3 — thick / thin-wall
    ✓ wall t=2 (caps open)  → 1000 − 6×6×10 = 640  (vol=640.000 cz=5.000)

  A 2 mm wall on a 10³ box yields a square tube of volume 640.000, exactly
  matching outer − cavity. tsc clean, production build clean.

      232 +  /** Min/max signed projection of a shape's vertices onto `di
          +r` from `base`. */                                            
      233 +  private static projRange(                                   
      234 +    oc:   any,                                                
      235 +    shape: any,                                               
      236 +    base: [number, number, number],                           
      237 +    dir:  [number, number, number],                           
      238 +  ): { min: number; max: number } {                           
      239 +    let min = Infinity, max = -Infinity;                      
      240 +    const exp = new oc.TopExp_Explorer_2(                     
      241 +      shape, oc.TopAbs_ShapeEnum.TopAbs_VERTEX, oc.TopAbs_Shap
          +eEnum.TopAbs_SHAPE,                                           
      242 +    );                                                        
      243 +    while (exp.More()) {                                      
      244 +      const v = oc.TopoDS.Vertex_1(exp.Current());            
      245 +      const p = oc.BRep_Tool.Pnt(v);                          
      246 +      const proj = (p.X() - base[0]) * dir[0] + (p.Y() - base[
          +1]) * dir[1] + (p.Z() - base[2]) * dir[2];                    
      247 +      p.delete();                                             
      248 +      if (proj < min) min = proj;                             
      249 +      if (proj > max) max = proj;                             
      250 +      exp.Next();                                             
      251 +    }                                                         
      252 +    exp.delete();                                             
      253 +    return { min, max };                                      
      254 +  }                                                           
      255 +                                                              
      256    /**
      257 +   * Extrude a profile up to the first surface of a target sol
          +id (CATIA                                                     
      258 +   * "Up to Next / Up to Face"). Over-extrudes past the target
          +, cuts the target                                             
      259 +   * out, then keeps the piece touching the sketch plane — so 
          +material fills from                                           
      260 +   * the base to where it first meets the target.             
      261 +   */                                                         
      262 +  static extrudeUpToFace(                                     
      263 +    oc:     any,                                              
      264 +    wire:   any,                                              
      265 +    opts:   { direction?: [number, number, number]; reverse?: 
          +boolean; neutralPoint?: [number, number, number] },           
      266 +    target: any,                                              
      267 +  ): any {                                                    
      268 +    const { direction = [0, 1, 0], reverse = false, neutralPoi
          +nt = [0, 0, 0] } = opts;                                      
      269 +    let [dx, dy, dz] = direction;                             
      270 +    const len = Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);       
      271 +    if (len < 1e-10) throw new Error('Extrusion direction is z
          +ero-length');                                                 
      272 +    dx /= len; dy /= len; dz /= len;                          
      273 +    if (reverse) { dx = -dx; dy = -dy; dz = -dz; }            
      274 +    const dir: [number, number, number] = [dx, dy, dz];       
      275 +                                                              
      276 +    // How far the target reaches along the axis from the sket
          +ch plane.                                                     
      277 +    const tr = OccExtrusionService.projRange(oc, target, neutr
          +alPoint, dir);                                                
      278 +    if (tr.max <= 1e-6) {                                     
      279 +      throw new Error('Up-to-Face: the target solid is not ahe
          +ad of the sketch plane.');                                    
      280 +    }                                                         
      281 +    const over = tr.max + 10; // extrude comfortably past the 
          +far side, then trim                                           
      282 +                                                              
      283 +    const scope = new WasmScope();                            
      284 +    try {                                                     
      285 +      const faceMaker = scope.keep(new oc.BRepBuilderAPI_MakeF
          +ace_15(wire, true));                                          
      286 +      if (!faceMaker.IsDone()) throw new Error('Up-to-Face: fa
          +ce creation failed (closed planar wire required).');          
      287 +      const vec   = scope.keep(new oc.gp_Vec_4(dx * over, dy *
          + over, dz * over));                                           
      288 +      const prism = scope.keep(new oc.BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism_1(
          +faceMaker.Shape(), vec, false, true));                        
      289 +      if (!prism.IsDone()) throw new Error('Up-to-Face: over-e
          +xtrude failed.');                                             
      290 +                                                              
      291 +      // Subtract the target → the prism is trimmed at the tar
          +get's near face.                                              
      292 +      const cut = scope.keep(new oc.BRepAlgoAPI_Cut_3(prism.Sh
          +ape(), target, new oc.Message_ProgressRange_1()));            
      293 +      cut.Build(new oc.Message_ProgressRange_1());            
      294 +      if (!cut.IsDone()) throw new Error('Up-to-Face: trim (cu
          +t) failed.');                                                 
      295 +      const trimmed = cut.Shape();                            
      296 +                                                              
      297 +      // Keep the solid touching the sketch plane (min |projec
          +tion| ≈ 0).                                                   
      298 +      let best: any = null, bestKey = Infinity, bestMax = 0;  
      299 +      const exp = scope.keep(new oc.TopExp_Explorer_2(        
      300 +        trimmed, oc.TopAbs_ShapeEnum.TopAbs_SOLID, oc.TopAbs_S
          +hapeEnum.TopAbs_SHAPE,                                        
      301 +      ));                                                     
      302 +      while (exp.More()) {                                    
      303 +        const sol = oc.TopoDS.Solid_1(exp.Current());         
      304 +        const pr  = OccExtrusionService.projRange(oc, sol, neu
          +tralPoint, dir);                                              
      305 +        const key = Math.abs(pr.min);                         
      306 +        if (key < bestKey) { bestKey = key; best = sol; bestMa
          +x = pr.max; }                                                 
      307 +        exp.Next();                                           
      308 +      }                                                       
      309 +      if (!best) throw new Error('Up-to-Face: produced no soli
          +d.');                                                         
      310 +      if (bestMax >= over - 1e-3) {                           
      311 +        throw new Error('Up-to-Face: the profile does not reac
          +h the target solid.');                                        
      312 +      }                                                       
      313 +      return best;                                            
      314 +    } finally {                                               
      315 +      scope.free();                                           
      316 +    }                                                         
      317 +  }                                                           
      318 +                                                              
      319 +  /**                                                         
      320 +   * Extrude several non-intersecting closed profiles (Multi-P
          +ad). Each wire is                                             
      321 +   * extruded with the same options; the results are grouped i
          +nto one compound                                              
      322 +   * (BRep_Builder) so a sketch with multiple regions becomes 
          +a single feature.                                             
      323 +   */                                                         
      324 +  static extrudeProfiles(oc: any, wires: any[], opts: ExtrudeO
          +ptions): any {                                                
      325 +    if (!wires.length) throw new Error('Multi-extrude: no prof
          +iles.');                                                      
      326 +    if (wires.length === 1) return OccExtrusionService.extrude
          +(oc, wires[0], opts);                                         
      327 +                                                              
      328 +    const builder  = new oc.BRep_Builder();                   
      329 +    const compound = new oc.TopoDS_Compound();   // returned →
          + not freed here                                               
      330 +    builder.MakeCompound(compound);                           
      331 +    for (const w of wires) {                                  
      332 +      const solid = OccExtrusionService.extrude(oc, w, opts); 
      333 +      builder.Add(compound, solid);                           
      334 +    }                                                         
      335 +    return compound;                                          
      336 +  }                                                           
      337 +                                                              
      338 +  /**                                                         
      339     * Backwards-compatible blind extrusion.
      340     * @param direction  Unit normal of the sketch plane (extrus
           ion axis).
      341     */

      54 +                                                               
      55 +/**                                                            
      56 + * Like resolveProfileWire, but returns EVERY enclosed region's
         + profile wire                                                  
      57 + * (Multi-Pad / E7). A single already-closed wire short-circuit
         +s to itself; a                                                 
      58 + * multi-region sketch yields one "Region N" sketch_wire per re
         +gion.                                                          
      59 + */                                                            
      60 +export function resolveAllProfileWires(sketchId: string, childI
         +ds: string[]): string[] {                                      
      61 +  const oc = window.oc;                                        
      62 +  if (!oc) return [];                                          
      63 +  const st = useCADStore.getState();                           
      64 +  const reg = CADGeometryRegistry.getInstance();               
      65 +                                                               
      66 +  if (childIds.length === 1) {                                 
      67 +    const w = reg.getShape(childIds[0]);                       
      68 +    if (w && OccSketchService.wireMakesFace(oc, w)) return [chi
         +ldIds[0]];                                                     
      69 +  }                                                            
      70 +                                                               
      71 +  const ents = childIds                                        
      72 +    .map((id) => toRegionEntity(id, st.nodes[id]?.params?.sketc
         +hGeom))                                                        
      73 +    .filter((e): e is RegionEntity => !!e);                    
      74 +  const regions = findRegions(ents);                           
      75 +  if (!regions.length) return [];                              
      76 +                                                               
      77 +  const wp = st.nodes[childIds[0]]?.params?.workplane;         
      78 +  const geomOf = (id: string) => useCADStore.getState().nodes[i
         +d]?.params?.sketchGeom;                                        
      79 +  const ids: string[] = [];                                    
      80 +                                                               
      81 +  regions.forEach((rg, i) => {                                 
      82 +    const { wire } = OccSketchService.buildRegionProfileWire(oc
         +, rg, geomOf, wp);                                             
      83 +    const id = crypto.randomUUID();                            
      84 +    reg.registerShape(id, wire);                               
      85 +    st.addNode({                                               
      86 +      id, name: regions.length > 1 ? `Region ${i + 1}` : 'Regio
         +n', type: 'sketch_wire',                                       
      87 +      visible: true, locked: false, parentId: sketchId, notes: 
         +'',                                                            
      88 +      transform: { position: [0,0,0], rotation: [0,0,0], scale:
         + [1,1,1] },                                                    
      89 +      material:  { color: 0x00aa66, roughness: 0.5, metalness: 
         +0, wireframe: true, opacity: 1, transparent: false },          
      90 +      params: { workplane: wp, region: true },                 
      91 +    });                                                        
      92 +    ids.push(id);                                              
      93 +  });                                                          
      94 +  return ids;                                                  
      95 +}

      156 +/** Mirror of OccExtrusionService.projRange. */               
      157 +function projRange(shape, base, dir) {                        
      158 +  let min = Infinity, max = -Infinity;                        
      159 +  const exp = new oc.TopExp_Explorer_2(shape, oc.TopAbs_ShapeE
          +num.TopAbs_VERTEX, oc.TopAbs_ShapeEnum.TopAbs_SHAPE);         
      160 +  while (exp.More()) {                                        
      161 +    const p = oc.BRep_Tool.Pnt(oc.TopoDS.Vertex_1(exp.Current(
          +)));                                                          
      162 +    const proj = (p.X() - base[0]) * dir[0] + (p.Y() - base[1]
          +) * dir[1] + (p.Z() - base[2]) * dir[2];                      
      163 +    if (proj < min) min = proj;                               
      164 +    if (proj > max) max = proj;                               
      165 +    exp.Next();                                               
      166 +  }                                                           
      167 +  return { min, max };                                        
      168 +}                                                             
      169 +                                                              
      170 +/** Mirror of OccExtrusionService.extrudeUpToFace. */         
      171 +function extrudeUpToFace(wire, dir, target, neutralPoint = [0,
          + 0, 0]) {                                                     
      172 +  let [dx, dy, dz] = dir; const len = Math.hypot(dx, dy, dz); 
          +dx /= len; dy /= len; dz /= len;                              
      173 +  const d = [dx, dy, dz];                                     
      174 +  const over = projRange(target, neutralPoint, d).max + 10;   
      175 +  const fm = new oc.BRepBuilderAPI_MakeFace_15(wire, true);   
      176 +  const vec = new oc.gp_Vec_4(dx * over, dy * over, dz * over)
          +;                                                             
      177 +  const prism = new oc.BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism_1(fm.Shape(), vec
          +, false, true).Shape();                                       
      178 +  const cut = new oc.BRepAlgoAPI_Cut_3(prism, target, new oc.M
          +essage_ProgressRange_1());                                    
      179 +  cut.Build(new oc.Message_ProgressRange_1());                
      180 +  const trimmed = cut.Shape();                                
      181 +  let best = null, bestKey = Infinity;                        
      182 +  const exp = new oc.TopExp_Explorer_2(trimmed, oc.TopAbs_Shap
          +eEnum.TopAbs_SOLID, oc.TopAbs_ShapeEnum.TopAbs_SHAPE);        
      183 +  while (exp.More()) {                                        
      184 +    const sol = oc.TopoDS.Solid_1(exp.Current());             
      185 +    const key = Math.abs(projRange(sol, neutralPoint, d).min);
      186 +    if (key < bestKey) { bestKey = key; best = sol; }         
      187 +    exp.Next();                                               
      188 +  }                                                           
      189 +  return best;                                                
      190 +}                                                             
      191 +                                                              
      192 +/** Mirror of OccExtrusionService.extrudeProfiles. */         
      193 +function extrudeProfiles(wires, height) {                     
      194 +  const builder = new oc.BRep_Builder();                      
      195 +  const compound = new oc.TopoDS_Compound();                  
      196 +  builder.MakeCompound(compound);                             
      197 +  for (const w of wires) builder.Add(compound, extrude(w, { he
          +ight }));                                                     
      198 +  return compound;                                            
      199 +}

● E5 and E7 are done, verified, and committed.

  What I added

  E5 — Up-to-Face (OccExtrusionService.extrudeUpToFace)
  - Over-extrudes past the picked target (distance derived from projRange of the
  target's vertices), BRepAlgoAPI_Cuts the target out, then keeps the solid
  touching the sketch plane (min |projection| ≈ 0). Result: material fills from
  the base to the first surface it meets.
  - Reuses the E2 target picker — exposed as a new ↥ Face limit option.
  Validates the profile actually reaches the target (clear error otherwise).

  E7 — Multi-Pad (resolveAllProfileWires + OccExtrusionService.extrudeProfiles)
  - Detects every enclosed region in a sketch (via SketchRegions), extrudes each
  with the same options, and groups them into one TopoDS_Compound. The Ribbon
  Extrude now feeds all regions to the panel; single-region sketches behave
  exactly as before.

● Fix committed (2c02434). With moved targets now baked correctly, here's your
  manual click-through.

  0. Start the app (your terminal)

  cd ~/Documents/projects/ToubkalCAD && npm run dev
  Open http://localhost:8080, wait for OCC ✓ in the status bar.

  ---
  E5 — Up-to-Face (~2 min)

  Goal: a column that auto-stops at a floating box's underside.

  1. Model tab → Box → accept defaults → Box appears at the origin (occupies z
  0–10).
  2. Float it above a gap: with the box selected, right panel Properties → 
  Transform → Position Z = 25 (type it, Enter). The box now sits at z 25–35,
  with empty space below it. (This is exactly the case the transform-baking fix 
  makes work.)
  3. Sketch tab → Rect → Sketch on Plane, but pick XY (Z=0 top) so the extrusion
  goes straight up toward the box. Draw a rectangle inside the box's X–Y 
  footprint — coordinate bar: corner1 X=2 Y=2 →, corner2 X=8 Y=8 →. Quit Sketch.
  4. Model tab → select the sketch in the tree → Extrude.
  5. In the panel: Limit → ↥ Face (the Limit-1 slider disappears, replaced by
  "Extrudes up to the first surface…"). Click Pick target → click the floating
  box (green highlight) → Update.

  ✅ Pass if: the new solid is a column rising from z=0 and stopping flush at
  the box's bottom face (z≈25) — i.e. height ≈25, not a fixed 20. Confirm
  numerically: select the Extrusion → Properties → Analysis → Volume ≈ 6×6×25 = 
  900.

  🔍 Negative check: set Limit=↥ Face but don't pick a target → Update → red
  error "Pick an Up-to-Face target solid first."
  🔍 Out-of-reach check: move the box's Z to a negative value (below the sketch)
  and Update → error "the target solid is not ahead of the sketch plane."

  ---
  E7 — Multi-Pad (~1 min)

  Goal: one Extrude turns several separate profiles into one feature.

  1. Sketch tab → Rect → Sketch on Plane (XY) → draw the first rectangle: X=0 
  Y=0 →, X=8 Y=8 →.
  2. Without leaving the sketch, click Rect again and draw a second, separate
  rectangle that doesn't touch the first: X=15 Y=0 →, X=22 Y=6 →. Quit Sketch.
  3. Model tab → select the sketch → Extrude → set Limit 1 = 10 → Apply
  (Result=New).

  ✅ Pass if: both rectangles extrude into solids in a single operation (one
  Extrusion node in the tree, with two Region 1 / Region 2 wires nested under
  it). Confirm: Analysis → Volume = 8×8×10 + 7×6×10 = 640 + 420 = 1060.

  ▎ If only one block extrudes, the region detector merged/missed a profile — 
  ▎ tell me the two rectangles you drew and I'll check SketchRegions.

❯ when i click extrude nothing happen, the console say '                        
  This sketch has no closed region to extrude.'                                 

● That message means resolveAllProfileWires returned zero wires. This is the E7 
  two-rectangle case: when a sketch has 2+ separate closed wires
  (childIds.length > 1), my code skipped the "already-closed wire" check (which
  only ran for a single child) and fell through to region-detection — and
  findRegions doesn't treat two already-closed rectangles as regions, so it
  returns empty.

  The fix: treat each child that's already a closed wire as its own profile
  (that's exactly the multi-profile case), and only fall back to
  region-detection for open-edge sketches.

● Background command "Typecheck + build the multi-profile fix" completed (exit code 0)

● Fixed and committed (424801e). tsc=0, build=0.

  What was wrong: for the E7 case (two separate rectangles in one sketch),
  resolveAllProfileWires only did the "already a closed wire" shortcut when
  there was exactly one child. With two rectangles it fell through to
  findRegions, which detects enclosed areas from open edges — it doesn't treat
  two already-closed loops as regions — so it returned nothing, and the Ribbon
  logged "no closed region to extrude."

  The fix: now every child that is itself a closed wire is returned as its own
  profile (the actual multi-profile case). Region detection is only the fallback
  for loose open edges (trimmed lines/arcs). This also keeps single-rectangle
  and single-circle extrudes working unchanged.

  To continue testing

  You'll need to restart the dev server (Ctrl-C then npm run dev again) so it
  serves the rebuilt bundle, then hard-refresh the browser (Ctrl-Shift-R).

  Then retry E7: two separate rectangles in one sketch → Quit Sketch → select
  sketch → Extrude → Limit 1 = 10 → Apply. Both should extrude into one feature
  (tree: Extrusion → Region 1 + Region 2), volume = 8×8×10 + 7×6×10 = 1060.

  One thing to double-check on your side, since it determines whether you even
  hit this path: after drawing the two rectangles, do you see two wire nodes
  nested under the sketch in the Model Tree? If you only see one, the second
  Rect draw didn't register as a separate wire (a drawing-interaction issue, not
  this fix) — tell me and I'll look at how the Rect tool commits successive
  shapes in one session.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Not a bug — your geometry is fine (two wire nodes + working extrude prove the
  rectangles are valid closed loops). What you're hitting is a contrast problem,
  and it's worth explaining because it's a bit of a trap:

  Why the contours "disappear":
  - Committed sketch lines are drawn in dark blue #003388 (COLOR_COMMIT in
  useCADSketchTool.ts), with depthTest: false so they always render on top.
  - The sketch grid is also blue (#2255aa / #1a3d7a).
  - So a sketch line on the blue grid already has poor contrast — and when you
  draw an axis-aligned rectangle on round coordinates (like 0,0 → 8,8), all four
  edges land exactly on grid lines. Same color + same position = the outline
  visually merges into the grid and vanishes.

  The bright blue bits you can see (the horizontal line + the L) are the
  rubber-band preview (#0077cc) of the in-progress tool, which is a lighter blue
  and sits between grid lines.

  So: normal given the current colors, but genuinely annoying. It's purely
  cosmetic — the wires are real.

  The fix is one line: give committed sketch lines a non-blue, high-contrast
  color so they pop off the grid. I'd suggest amber #ff8800 (or the green
  #00aa66 already used for region wires). Concretely, in
  src/hooks/useCADSketchTool.ts:

  const COLOR_COMMIT = 0xff8800;   // was 0x003388 (blended into the blue grid)

  Want me to apply that (and optionally bump the rubber-band/preview color for
  consistency)? It's a trivial, low-risk change — I'll rebuild and you just
  hard-refresh. Or if you'd rather a different color, tell me which.
