      339 +  private static normDir(direction: [number, number, number], 
          +reverse: boolean): [number, number, number] {                 
      340 +    let [dx, dy, dz] = direction;                             
      341 +    const len = Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);       
      342 +    if (len < 1e-10) throw new Error('Extrusion direction is z
          +ero-length');                                                 
      343 +    dx /= len; dy /= len; dz /= len;                          
      344 +    return reverse ? [-dx, -dy, -dz] : [dx, dy, dz];          
      345 +  }

      424 +        if (!hasSolid) continue;

          +nge(oc, cs, neutralPoint, dir).max);

          +ofile does not reach any body.');

          +5(wire, true));                                               
      430 +      const vec2 = scope.keep(new oc.gp_Vec_4(dx * farMost, dy
          + * farMost, dz * farMost));

          + touching the

          +he full over-extrude

      442 +  private static keepBaseSolid(                               
      443 +    oc: any, shape: any, base: [number, number, number], dir: 
          +[number, number, number], over: number,                       
      444 +  ): any {                                                    
      445 +    let best: any = null, bestKey = Infinity, bestMax = 0;    
      446 +    const exp = new oc.TopExp_Explorer_2(shape, oc.TopAbs_Shap
          +eEnum.TopAbs_SOLID, oc.TopAbs_ShapeEnum.TopAbs_SHAPE);        
      447 +    while (exp.More()) {                                      
      448 +      const sol = oc.TopoDS.Solid_1(exp.Current());           
      449 +      const pr  = OccExtrusionService.projRange(oc, sol, base,
          + dir);                                                        
      450 +      const key = Math.abs(pr.min);                           
      451 +      if (key < bestKey) { bestKey = key; best = sol; bestMax 
          += pr.max; }                                                   
      452 +      exp.Next();                                             
      453 +    }                                                         
      454 +    exp.delete();                                             
      455 +    if (!best) throw new Error('Up-to: produced no solid.');  
      456 +    if (bestMax >= over - 1e-3) throw new Error('Up-to: the pr
          +ofile does not reach the target.');                           
      457 +    return best;                                              
      458 +  }

● E6 is done — the entire E-track (E1–E7) is now complete. 13/13 headless, tsc
  clean, build clean, committed (68861a3).

  E6 — Up-to-Next / Up-to-Last

  Unlike E5 (pick one target), these scan every body in the scene automatically:
  - extrudeUpToNext — over-extrude, cut all bodies, keep the base-connected
  piece → stops at the nearest surface (shares keepBaseSolid with E5).
  - extrudeUpToLast — intersect the over-extrude with each body
  (BRepAlgoAPI_Common) to find the furthest exit, then blind-extrude to it
  (fills gaps).
  - Both use getPlacedShape, so moved bodies count where you see them. UI: Next
  / Last limit options (the toggle now wraps to fit 6 modes).

  E6 — up to next / last
    ✓ up-to-next (nearest z20)  → z[0,20] = 2000
    ✓ up-to-last (furthest z50) → z[0,50] = 5000

  The extrusion tool — full CATIA Pad/Pocket parity

❯ manual E6 scenario                                                            

● Here's the E6 click-through. It needs two bodies at different distances so you
  can see "Next" stop at the near one and "Last" reach the far one.

  0. Reload first

  Restart the dev server (Ctrl-C, npm run dev) and hard-refresh (Ctrl-Shift-R)
  so you get the E6 build.

  1. Build two floating targets (~1 min)

  1. Model tab → Box → accept defaults. Select it → Properties → Transform → 
  Position Z = 20 (Box A now floats at z 20–30).
  2. Model tab → Box again → select it → Position Z = 40 (Box B floats at z
  40–50).

  Both boxes share the same X–Y footprint (0–10), stacked with a gap and clear
  air below.

  2. Sketch a profile under them

  3. Sketch tab → Rect → Sketch on Plane → XY (so it extrudes straight up toward
  the boxes). Draw inside the footprint: coordinate bar X=2 Y=2 →, X=8 Y=8 → (a
  6×6 square). Quit Sketch.

  3. Extrude — toggle Next vs Last in the live preview

  4. Model tab → select the sketch → Extrude.
  5. Set Limit → Next: the preview column rises from z=0 and stops just under 
  Box A (height ≈20).
  6. Switch Limit → Last: the same column now shoots up to the top of Box B
  (height ≈50), passing through both boxes.
  7. Pick whichever you want and Apply (Result = New).

  ✅ Pass criteria — confirm numerically

  Select the resulting Extrusion → Properties → Analysis → Volume:

  ┌───────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │ Limit │                  Result                   │ Volume (6×6 profile) │
  ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Next  │ column z[0,20] (stops at nearest body)    │ 720                  │
  ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Last  │ column z[0,50] (reaches furthest surface) │ 1800                 │
  └───────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

  The key visual tell: Next ≠ Last — Next is short (under Box A), Last is tall
  (through both to Box B's top). That difference is the whole feature.

  🔍 Edge checks (optional but valuable)

  - No targets: delete both boxes, try Limit=Next → red error "Up-to-Next/Last 
  needs another solid in the model."
  - Behind the plane: set a box's Z to a negative value (below the sketch) and
  Next/Last → "no body ahead of the sketch plane."
  - Moved-body correctness: this scenario only works because the gizmo move is
  baked (the getPlacedShape fix) — if Next stopped at z=10 instead of z=20, the
  move wasn't baked. It should stop at 20.

  ---
  One caveat worth knowing: with Result = New, the "Last" column visually passes
  through Boxes A and B (they intersect, no fusing). That's expected — to merge
  them into one body you'd set Result = Pad and pick a target, which combines
  Up-to-Last with the boolean. For just confirming E6 geometry, New + the volume
  readouts (720 / 1800) is the cleanest check.

  Report back the two volumes and we'll know E6 is solid end-to-end. Want me to
  push the branch after this?

❯ now let's move on , we have a tool9button0 in toolbar that create sketch      
  plane , it's working fine but the problem if for custom plane , it requires   
  point and vector to create plane which is inhabitual in cad software,         
  professional cad had set uo plane based on options . here one found for       
  fusion 360, it containt also references geometry like points, axis to         
  reference our created parts, it also containt opencascade counterpart to this 
  fusion 360 refernce geometry. i want you to chech this document and make a    
  roadmap to implement those features, i want also to make my reference plane   
  look like those found in standard cad software. on last point, when we create 
  those reference geometries esppecially reference plane, it's not necessary    
  to sketch on them, once created, they can be created and appears in tree      
  panel as plane 1/plane2 until we sketch on them. take a look at this          
  document[In Autodesk Fusion 360, reference geometry falls into two main       
  categories: Construction Geometry (work planes, axes, and points used to      
  build 3D shapes) and Projected/Derived Geometry (existing edges and sketches  
  brought into a new workspace for alignment and associative modeling). [1, 2,  
  3]                                                                            
  1. Construction Geometry (Work Features)                                      
  Used to create features or sketches in mid-air or at odd angles where no      
  standard origin plane exists. [1]                                             
  Planes: Found under Construct, these include Offset Plane, Plane at Angle,    
  and Tangent Plane. You can create custom planes to sketch angled geometry.    
  Axes: Located via Construct > Axis, these allow you to create center lines    
  through cylinders or cones, or intersection lines between two planes.         
  Points: Found via Construct > Point, these mark exact locations in 3D space,  
  useful for locating reference points or hole centers.                         
  2. Projecting & Including Geometry                                            
  Instead of manually recreating the dimensions of existing parts, you can      
  reference surrounding features.                                               
  Project (Hotkey: P): Projects 3D edges, faces, or points onto your active     
  sketch plane. This projected geometry is associative—meaning if the original  
  body changes shape, your sketch updates automatically.                        
  Intersect: Similar to Project, but generates lines only where the sketch      
  plane slices through a 3D body.                                               
  Include: Brings existing 3D curves or sketch lines into your current sketch,  
  primarily used for complex 3D curves in lofts and sweeps.                     
                                                                                
  OpenCascade.js replicates Fusion 360’s reference geometry using mathematical  
  primitives (the gp_ package) and topological algorithms. Because it is        
  ──── (94 lines hidden) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  to match XY plane                                                             
    const gridDivisions = 10;                                                   
    const gridHelper = new THREE.GridHelper(size, gridDivisions, 0xd47a00,      
  0xb56300);                                                                    
    gridHelper.rotation.x = Math.PI / 2;                                        
    gridHelper.material.transparent = true;                                     
    gridHelper.material.opacity = 0.4;                                          
                                                                                
    // Assign a name to find it easily in the render loop                       
    gridHelper.name = "sketchGrid";                                             
    gridHelper.visible = false; // Start hidden                                 
    planeGroup.add(gridHelper);                                                 
                                                                                
    planeGroup.applyMatrix4(transformMatrix);                                   
    return planeGroup;                                                          
  }                                                                             
  Step 3:. Add the Visibility Check to your Render Loop                         
  Create a validation function. Run it inside your requestAnimationFrame loop.  
  It compares the camera's view direction vector with the plane's normal vector 
  using a geometric dot product.                                                
  /**                                                                           
   * Updates grid visibility based on app state and camera angle.               
   * @param {THREE.Group} planeGroup - The plane object group                   
   * @param {THREE.Camera} camera - Your Three.js active camera                 
   * @param {boolean} isSketchMode - App state flag                             
   */                                                                           
  function updateGridVisibility(planeGroup, camera, isSketchMode) {             
    const grid = planeGroup.getObjectByName("sketchGrid");                      
    if (!grid) return;                                                          
                                                                                
    // Rule 1: Must be in sketch mode                                           
    if (!isSketchMode) {                                                        
      grid.visible = false;                                                     
      return;                                                                   
    }                                                                           
                                                                                
    // 1. Get world normal vector of the plane (Local Z-axis is 0,0,1)          
    const planeNormal = new THREE.Vector3(0, 0, 1);                             
    planeNormal.applyMatrix4(planeGroup.matrixWorld).normalize();               
                                                                                
    // 2. Get camera look direction vector                                      
    const cameraDir = new THREE.Vector3();                                      
    camera.getWorldDirection(cameraDir);                                        
                                                                                
    // 3. Compute dot product (1.0 = perfectly parallel/normal, 0.0 =           
  perpendicular)                                                                
    const dotProduct = Math.abs(cameraDir.dot(planeNormal));                    
                                                                                
    // 4. Set angular threshold (0.995 equals roughly within 5 degrees of       
  dead-on normal)                                                               
    const normalThreshold = 0.995;                                              
                                                                                
    if (dotProduct >= normalThreshold) {                                        
      grid.visible = true;                                                      
                                                                                
      // Optional: Smoothly fade grid in as camera approaches perfect normal    
  view                                                                          
      const factor = (dotProduct - normalThreshold) / (1.0 - normalThreshold);  
      grid.material.opacity = factor * 0.5;                                     
    } else {                                                                    
      grid.visible = false;                                                     
    }                                                                           
  }                                                                             
  Step 4:. Usage Example                                                        
  let isSketchMode = true; // Toggle this with your UI buttons                  
                                                                                
  function animate() {                                                          
    requestAnimationFrame(animate);                                             
                                                                                
    // Run the visibility calculation every frame                               
    updateGridVisibility(myPlaneGroup, camera, isSketchMode);                   
                                                                                
    renderer.render(scene, camera);                                             
  }

● Roadmap written to docs/REFERENCE-GEOMETRY.md (with a pointer added in
  ROADMAP.md as Track D). Here's the gist.

  The core shift you asked for

  Right now planes aren't entities — PlaneSelector.confirm() calls
  startSketchSession() immediately, so a plane only exists for the sketch it
  spawns, and "custom" means typing a raw origin+normal. The roadmap decouples 
  create from sketch: a datum plane is made via an option-based command, lands
  in the tree as Plane 1 / Plane 2, renders persistently, and you sketch on it
  later (right-click → Create Sketch) — which the web confirms is exactly how
  Fusion/SolidWorks/CATIA work.

  What I verified (so the plan is real, not theoretical)

  - All the OCC classes exist in our opencascade.js build: gce_MakePln_1..8,
  gp_Pln.Position()→gp_Ax3 / Rotated / Translated,
  gp_Ax3.Location/Direction/XDirection/YDirection, plus GeomLProp_SLProps,
  BRepProj_Projection, BRepAlgoAPI_Section, BRepBuilderAPI_MakeVertex.
  - Big reuse wins already in the codebase: Workplane is already the exact plane
  payload; startSketchSession(wp) already starts a sketch from any workplane;
  useCADSketchFacePick is the template for pick-a-reference-and-derive-a-plane;
  and the doc's depthWrite:false amber-plane trick is one we already use for the
  workplane fill and S2 face overlays — so the Fusion look is proven-feasible
  here.

  Track D — phased (full detail + OCC cheat-sheet in the doc)

  - D0 (keystone) — datum node model (datum_plane/axis/point NodeTypes) +
  persistent Fusion-style amber rendering (translucent face #f0a30a + border
  #d47a00, bbox-sized, grid only in sketch mode via the |camera·normal|>0.995
  gate). Delivers both the look and the decoupling.
  - D1 + D9 — origin datums (XY/YZ/ZX) you can sketch on later = the full
  create→tree→sketch loop.
  - D2 Offset → D4 Three-Point → D5 Midplane → D3 Angle — the high-value plane
  types.
  - D7 Axis + D8 Point — references for angle planes, hole centers, pattern
  axes.
  - D6 advanced planes (tangent/along-path), D11/D12 Project/Intersect, D10
  retire the raw point+vector UI, D13 associativity (needs P1 feature tree).

  A few honest notes

  - This is a bigger subsystem than the E-track — D0 alone is ~3+ days because
  it adds a whole new render path and node category. It's not headlessly
  volume-testable the way extrusion was; correctness is mostly visual + the
  gp_Ax3→Workplane mapping, so it'll lean more on the manual click-through style
  you've been doing.
  - I corrected one doc assumption: datum planes need no CADGeometryRegistry 
  solid — they render straight from params (only Project/Section/sketch-on need
  a bounded MakeFace).

  Docs are written but not committed (and they're unrelated to the
  feat/extrusion-advanced branch). Want me to (a) commit the roadmap — on main
  or a fresh docs branch, (b) start implementing D0, or (c) adjust priorities
  first?

      46 +// ─── Datum-plane visual (Track D, D0) ───────────────────────
         +──────────────────                                             
      47 +// Fusion-style construction plane: translucent amber face + da
         +rker border,                                                   
      48 +// oriented from the node's workplane (u/v/normal). depthWrite:
         +false so it never                                              
      49 +// clips solids behind it. Tagged datumNodeId (not cadNodeId) s
         +o the solid pick                                               
      50 +// hooks ignore it.                                            
      51 +const DATUM_SIZE = 100;                                        
      52 +function buildDatumPlaneGroup(id: string, wp: {                
      53 +  origin: [number,number,number]; normal: [number,number,number
         +];                                                             
      54 +  uAxis: [number,number,number]; vAxis: [number,number,number];
      55 +}): THREE.Group {                                              
      56 +  const u = new THREE.Vector3(...wp.uAxis).normalize();        
      57 +  const v = new THREE.Vector3(...wp.vAxis).normalize();        
      58 +  const n = new THREE.Vector3(...wp.normal).normalize();       
      59 +  const o = new THREE.Vector3(...wp.origin);                   
      60  
      61 +  const geo  = new THREE.PlaneGeometry(DATUM_SIZE, DATUM_SIZE);
      62 +  const face = new THREE.Mesh(geo, new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial(
         +{                                                              
      63 +    color: 0xf0a30a, side: THREE.DoubleSide, transparent: true,
         + opacity: 0.16, depthWrite: false,                             
      64 +  }));                                                         
      65 +  const border = new THREE.LineSegments(                       
      66 +    new THREE.EdgesGeometry(geo),                              
      67 +    new THREE.LineBasicMaterial({ color: 0xd47a00, transparent:
         + true, opacity: 0.85, depthWrite: false }),                    
      68 +  );                                                           
      69 +  const group = new THREE.Group();                             
      70 +  group.add(face, border);                                     
      71 +  group.applyMatrix4(new THREE.Matrix4().makeBasis(u, v, n).set
         +Position(o)); // local X→u, Y→v, Z→n                           
      72 +  group.userData.datumNodeId = id;                             
      73 +  group.renderOrder = 997;                                     
      74 +  return group;                                                
      75 +}                                                              
      76 +                                                               
      77 +function disposeGroup(g: THREE.Object3D) {                     
      78 +  g.traverse((o) => {                                          
      79 +    const m = o as THREE.Mesh;                                 
      80 +    if (m.geometry) m.geometry.dispose();                      
      81 +    if (m.material) (Array.isArray(m.material) ? m.material : [
         +m.material]).forEach((x) => x.dispose());                      
      82 +  });                                                          
      83 +}                                                              
      84 +                                                               
      85 +

           }) => {

● D9 is done and committed (a265657).

  What's now possible

  The full create → tree → sketch-later loop the user wanted:
  1. Model ▸ Datum ▸ Origin Planes (or Offset Plane) → reference planes appear
  in the tree as Plane N, rendered as persistent amber Fusion-style planes.
  2. Right-click a plane → "Create Sketch" → starts a sketch on it (camera snaps
  normal-on, Line tool active). Reliable path, verified headless.
  3. Sketch ▸ Datum ▸ "On Datum" → enter pick mode, click a plane in the
  viewport → same result. CAD-feel path, wired but needs a manual click-test 
  (raycast picks don't drive headlessly).

  Both routes funnel through the existing startSketchSession, so the sketch
  grid, camera framing, and downstream extrude/etc. all work unchanged.

  Verified: right-click Plane 1 [XY] → Create Sketch → Sketch 1 [XY] ACTIVE in
  tree, Line tool live, console "Sketching on Plane 1 [XY].", zero errors.
  tsc/build clean.

● Right — that's the missing piece. Here's why you don't see the work on the
  repo's main page:

  main only got the CLAUDE.md commit. All the real work (extrusion E1–E7, datum
  D0/D9, roadmaps) is on the two feature branches I pushed. On GitHub those are
  visible only via the branch dropdown (top-left, "main ▾" → pick
  feat/extrusion-advanced or feat/reference-geometry) or by opening a PR — not
  on the default main view.

  To get everything onto main, you don't need a PR — the branches are stacked
  linearly on top of main, so it's a clean fast-forward. feat/reference-geometry
  already contains all of it (extrusion + datum + roadmaps), so one merge
  brings the lot:

  git checkout main
  git merge --ff-only feat/reference-geometry
  git push origin main

  After that, main will show all the extrusion + datum work. (--ff-only
  guarantees it's a clean fast-forward with no merge commit; if it refuses, the
  branches diverged and we'd look closer — but they shouldn't have.)

  You can run that in your terminal, or type ! git checkout main && git merge 
  --ff-only feat/reference-geometry && git push origin main here and I'll see
  the output. Want me to run it for you instead?

  One caveat: this puts everything straight on main with no review step. If
  you'd rather keep main reviewable, open the two PRs from the branch dropdown
  instead and merge them there. Your call — which way do you want it?
